    *   **Dependence on specific datasets and models:**  The performance might be sensitive to the choice of the HSFace dataset, the face recognition model used for embedding extraction, and the Stable Diffusion model. However, these are reasonable starting points.
    *   **Evaluation limited to the DataCV challenge:**  While winning the challenge is a strong positive, it would be beneficial to see a more extensive evaluation on other datasets and FR architectures.
    *   **Computational cost**: While Vec2Face reduces some of the cost, generating initial images still requires Stable Diffusion which is computationally intensive. This can limit the scalability of the method.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper is likely to influence future research in privacy-preserving face recognition and synthetic data generation. The hybrid approach and curriculum learning strategy offer valuable insights for others working in this area. The successful demonstration of the method in a competitive setting will encourage further exploration of similar techniques.

*   **Justification for Score:**
    I'm assigning a score of 7. The paper presents a solid engineering solution to a relevant problem, and the results are compelling. The novelty lies in the effective combination of existing techniques and the practical demonstration of its effectiveness. The paper shows significant improvement from the benchmark, showing its ability to train robust FR models. However, the individual components are not groundbreaking, and a more extensive evaluation would strengthen the claims. The approach is well-suited to the current landscape of face recognition research. The computational cost makes it less general.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Advancing Autonomous Incident Response: Leveraging LLMs and Cyber Threat Intelligence
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.10677v1)
- **Authors**: Amine Tellache, Abdelaziz Amara Korba, Amdjed Mokhtari, Horea Moldovan, Yacine Ghamri-Doudane
- **Abstract**: Effective incident response (IR) is critical for mitigating cyber threats, yet security teams are overwhelmed by alert fatigue, high false-positive rates, and the vast volume of unstructured Cyber Threat Intelligence (CTI) documents. While CTI holds immense potential for enriching security operations, its extensive and fragmented nature makes manual analysis time-consuming and resource-intensive. To bridge this gap, we introduce a novel Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG)-based framework that leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) to automate and enhance IR by integrating dynamically retrieved CTI. Our approach introduces a hybrid retrieval mechanism that combines NLP-based similarity searches within a CTI vector database with standardized queries to external CTI platforms, facilitating context-aware enrichment of security alerts. The augmented intelligence is then leveraged by an LLM-powered response generation module, which formulates precise, actionable, and contextually relevant incident mitigation strategies. We propose a dual evaluation paradigm, wherein automated assessment using an auxiliary LLM is systematically cross-validated by cybersecurity experts. Empirical validation on real-world and simulated alerts demonstrates that our approach enhances the accuracy, contextualization, and efficiency of IR, alleviating analyst workload and reducing response latency. This work underscores the potential of LLM-driven CTI fusion in advancing autonomous security operations and establishing a foundation for intelligent, adaptive cybersecurity frameworks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG)-based framework to automate and enhance incident response (IR) by leveraging Large Language Models (LLMs) and Cyber Threat Intelligence (CTI). The framework combines NLP-based similarity searches within a CTI vector database with standardized queries to external CTI platforms for context-aware alert enrichment. An LLM-powered module then generates precise, actionable mitigation strategies. The system is evaluated using a dual approach: automated assessment via a LLM and cross-validation by cybersecurity experts, using both real-world and simulated alerts.  The results suggest the framework enhances accuracy, contextualization, and efficiency in IR.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The core idea of using LLMs with RAG for incident response is not entirely novel.  Several prior works have explored LLMs for threat intelligence and security automation. However, the paper's novelty lies in its specific combination of techniques and its rigorous evaluation methodology. The hybrid retrieval mechanism (NLP similarity + standardized queries) is a valuable contribution.  The dual evaluation paradigm (LLM-based automated scoring + human expert validation) is particularly strong and addresses a significant concern regarding the reliability of LLM-generated outputs in a security-critical context.  The focus on *continuous integration* of new CTI data *without* requiring model fine-tuning, which is vital for keeping the system up to date, is another relevant aspect.

**Significance:**

The paper addresses a genuine and crucial problem: the increasing complexity of incident response and the strain on security teams.  Automating the process of CTI integration and response generation has the potential to significantly reduce alert fatigue, improve response times, and enhance the overall effectiveness of cybersecurity operations. The paper's focus on actionable insights for mitigation provides more practical value than solutions focused solely on threat detection or summarization. The practical experiments using real-world and simulated alerts contribute to the paper's real-world relevance.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:**  The paper clearly articulates the challenges in modern incident response.
*   **Well-Defined Approach:** The RAG framework is clearly described, with attention to detail in the retrieval and generation stages.
*   **Rigorous Evaluation:** The dual evaluation framework is a strong point and helps validate the results. The use of both real-world and simulated data strengthens the findings.
*   **Practical Focus:**  The emphasis on actionable responses and continuous CTI integration addresses practical needs in security operations.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope of CTI Integration:** The reliance primarily on VirusTotal and the VX Underground APT repository limits the generality of the findings. Integrating more diverse CTI sources would enhance the robustness of the system.
*   **Lack of Comparative Evaluation:**  While the paper demonstrates the effectiveness of *its* approach, it lacks a direct comparison with other existing methods for automated incident response or CTI enrichment. A benchmark against traditional methods (e.g., rule-based systems) or other LLM-based approaches would strengthen the claims.
*   **LLM Hallucination Mitigation:**  While the evaluation includes 'groundedness', it's not clear how the system specifically *mitigates* LLM hallucinations during the generation stage. Error handling should be discussed.
*   **Security Against Adversarial Attacks on LLM:** The resilience of this system against adversarial attacks on the LLM (prompt injections, etc.) is not discussed. In real-world implementations, this could be a major issue.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence the field of security automation.  It offers a viable approach for leveraging LLMs in incident response, especially highlighting the importance of continuous CTI integration and rigorous evaluation. It can serve as a foundation for building more intelligent and adaptive cybersecurity frameworks.

**Justification of Score:**

Considering the strengths and weaknesses, I am assigning a score of **7**. The paper presents a solid and practically relevant approach to a critical problem. The dual evaluation and continuous integration aspects contribute meaningfully. However, the limitations in the scope of CTI integration and the absence of comparative evaluation prevent it from achieving a higher score. The discussion of adversarial attacks on the LLM and halluciantion should be enhanced.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Novel View Synthesis using DDIM Inversion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.10688v1)
- **Authors**: Sehajdeep SIngh, A V Subramanyam
- **Abstract**: Synthesizing novel views from a single input image is a challenging task. It requires extrapolating the 3D structure of a scene while inferring details in occluded regions, and maintaining geometric consistency across viewpoints. Many existing methods must fine-tune large diffusion backbones using multiple views or train a diffusion model from scratch, which is extremely expensive. Additionally, they suffer from blurry reconstruction and poor generalization. This gap presents the opportunity to explore an explicit lightweight view translation framework that can directly utilize the high-fidelity generative capabilities of a pretrained diffusion model while reconstructing a scene from a novel view. Given the DDIM-inverted latent of a single input image, we employ a camera pose-conditioned translation U-Net, TUNet, to predict the inverted latent corresponding to the desired target view. However, the image sampled using the predicted latent may result in a blurry reconstruction. To this end, we propose a novel fusion strategy that exploits the inherent noise correlation structure observed in DDIM inversion. The proposed fusion strategy helps preserve the texture and fine-grained details. To synthesize the novel view, we use the fused latent as the initial condition for DDIM sampling, leveraging the generative prior of the pretrained diffusion model. Extensive experiments on MVImgNet demonstrate that our method outperforms existing methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper presents a novel view synthesis method from a single input image using DDIM inversion. The approach leverages a lightweight, view translation U-Net (TUNet) to predict the inverted latent representation of the target view, conditioned on camera pose. To address the blurry reconstructions often associated with diffusion model approaches, the authors propose a novel fusion strategy that exploits the noise correlation structure inherent in DDIM inversion, injecting high-frequency details. The fused latent is then used as the initial condition for DDIM sampling using a pre-trained diffusion model.  Experiments on MVImgNet demonstrate improved performance compared to existing methods. The paper highlights the efficiency of their approach, avoiding fine-tuning large diffusion models or training from scratch.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates some novelty in its specific combination of techniques, especially the fusion strategy to reintroduce high-frequency details lost during the DDIM inversion and subsequent translation. However, individual components are not entirely novel. DDIM inversion is a well-established technique, and U-Net architectures are commonly used for image translation tasks. The concept of conditioning a diffusion model with camera pose information has also been explored. The specific way the noise from DDIM inversion is re-integrated constitutes the primary novelty. The conditioning of the UNet with camera parameters can be viewed as an extension of existing methods.

*   **Significance:** The paper contributes to the field by presenting a more efficient approach to single-image novel view synthesis. Avoiding the computational cost of fine-tuning or training large diffusion models from scratch makes the approach more accessible. The achieved performance improvement, although demonstrated on MVImgNet, could make the method appealing for applications with limited computational resources or data. Demonstrating generalizability to out-of-domain images (Unsplash dataset) adds to the significance, suggesting robustness beyond controlled datasets. The main claim of generating 'high-quality' novel views requires further justification. Qualitative results show that images can sometimes look deformed, suggesting that geometry reconstruction can still be imperfect.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Efficiency:** The lightweight nature of the TUNet and reliance on a pre-trained diffusion model provide a significant computational advantage.
    *   **Fusion Strategy:** The proposed noise fusion technique addresses a crucial limitation of the DDIM inversion approach, improving reconstruction detail.
    *   **Generalizability:** Demonstrating effectiveness on out-of-domain images is a valuable contribution.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the fusion strategy is novel, the overall architecture combines existing techniques. The improvement might be marginal.
    *   **Limited Evaluation:** The evaluation focuses primarily on MVImgNet and the out-of-domain examples. The comparison with NViST can be improved with better hyperparameter optimisation for NViST. A comprehensive comparison against other state-of-the-art techniques is recommended. Additionally, reporting runtime (inference time) and comparing it against other approaches would reinforce the claim of efficiency.
    *   **Qualitative Imperfections:** The sample novel views still exhibit artifacts, suggesting limitations in geometric consistency.
    *   **Lack of Theoretical Justification:** While the noise correlation claim is made, there is little theoretical or mathematical justification for *why* the noise fusion works as well as it does beyond empirical evidence.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's influence will depend on whether the performance gains demonstrated on MVImgNet translate to other datasets and tasks. If the method proves to be more efficient without sacrificing significant visual quality, it could influence future research directions in novel view synthesis, encouraging more lightweight approaches.

**Score: 6**

**Justification:** The paper has notable strengths: its efficiency, the novel fusion strategy, and evidence of generalizability. However, it also suffers from limited novelty, requiring further experimentation. The lack of theoretical insights and remaining artifacts in generated views also temper the score. While a worthwhile contribution, it represents a moderate advancement rather than a transformative breakthrough.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Learning from Natural Language Feedback for Personalized Question Answering
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.10695v1)
- **Authors**: Alireza Salemi, Hamed Zamani
- **Abstract**: Personalization is crucial for enhancing both the effectiveness and user satisfaction of language technologies, particularly in information-seeking tasks like question answering. Current approaches for personalizing large language models (LLMs) often rely on retrieval-augmented generation (RAG), followed by reinforcement learning with scalar reward signals to teach models how to use retrieved personal context. We believe that these scalar rewards sometimes provide weak, non-instructive feedback, limiting learning efficiency and personalization quality. We introduce VAC, a novel framework for personalized response generation that replaces scalar rewards with natural language feedback (NLF) that are generated conditioned on the user profiles and the question narratives. NLF serves as a rich and actionable supervision signal, allowing the policy model to iteratively refine its outputs and internalize effective personalization strategies. Training alternates between optimizing the feedback model and fine-tuning the policy model on the improved responses, resulting in a policy model that no longer requires feedback at inference. Evaluation on the LaMP-QA benchmark that consists of three diverse domains demonstrates consistent and significant improvements over the state-of-the-art results. Human evaluations further confirm the superior quality of the generated responses. These results demonstrate that NLF provides more effective signals for optimizing personalized question answering.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces VAC, a novel framework for personalized question answering that utilizes natural language feedback (NLF) to train large language models (LLMs).  Instead of using scalar reward signals (common in reinforcement learning), VAC uses an LLM to generate NLF based on user profiles and question narratives. This NLF provides richer, actionable supervision for the policy model (the QA LLM), allowing it to refine its outputs and internalize personalization strategies. The framework alternates between optimizing the feedback model and fine-tuning the policy model.  Experiments on the LaMP-QA benchmark demonstrate that VAC outperforms state-of-the-art baselines in terms of personalization quality and inference efficiency. Human evaluations further validate the superior quality of VAC-generated responses.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:**  The core novelty lies in the use of LLM-generated natural language feedback for *personalized* question answering.  Prior work has used NLF in other contexts (e.g., code generation, math reasoning), but applying it to personalize a QA system, particularly in the RAG setting, is a significant step. The iterative training process, where the feedback model and policy model co-adapt, also contributes to the novelty.  Compared to RL-based personalization approaches that rely on scalar rewards, NLF promises more directed and efficient learning. Furthermore, it differs from collaborative setup where models communicate in feedback loops to solve specific problem instances, as the focus here is on *learning a policy* for personalized outputs. This distinction is important.

* **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from addressing limitations of existing personalized LLM approaches. Scalar rewards, while useful, are often weak and require substantial exploration. NLF provides a richer, more actionable signal, potentially leading to faster training and better personalization outcomes. The empirical results support this claim, demonstrating improvements over strong baselines.  The focus on inference efficiency is also important; VAC eliminates the need for feedback at inference time, maintaining the speed of a standard LLM. The human evaluation is also important for establishing user preference with respect to the proposed method.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition and Motivation:** The paper clearly articulates the shortcomings of scalar reward-based personalization and motivates the need for a more informative feedback signal.
    * **Well-Defined Framework:** VAC is a well-defined and implementable framework with a clear training procedure.
    * **Strong Empirical Results:**  The paper provides compelling experimental results on a relevant benchmark, showing significant improvements over state-of-the-art methods. The ablation studies offer valuable insights into the importance of different components of the framework (e.g., training the feedback model, choice of optimization method).
    * **Human Evaluation:** Inclusion of human evaluation strengthens the claim of improved personalization.
    * **Code and Data Release:** Publicly releasing code and data will facilitate further research and adoption of the framework.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Computational Cost:** While the paper emphasizes inference efficiency, the training process is computationally expensive, requiring multiple GPUs and significant training time. This limits accessibility and scalability, though future work might mitigate this with more parameter-efficient methods. The 750 GPU hours of compute time needed for training is substantial.
    * **LLM Dependence:**  The framework's performance hinges on the quality of the LLMs used for both the policy model and the feedback model. Future research should investigate how VAC performs with different LLM architectures and sizes.  The method may be less effective if the LLM struggles to generate high-quality, relevant feedback.
    * **Limited Scope of Personalization:** The personalization is based on past questions and descriptions. The effectiveness of this approach may be limited in scenarios where other forms of personalized data are available (e.g., demographics, interests, browsing history). It focuses on the user query understanding, not generation.

* **Potential Influence:** VAC has the potential to influence future research in personalized LLMs, particularly in question answering and other information-seeking tasks. The idea of using NLF for more effective training is promising and could be extended to other areas.  The framework could also inspire research on improving the quality and efficiency of LLM-generated feedback.

* **Justification of Score:** The paper presents a solid contribution with a novel approach, strong empirical validation, and clear potential for future research.  While the computational cost and LLM dependence are limitations, the improvements in personalization quality and inference efficiency are significant. The work addresses real challenges in the field.

### Chem3DLLM: 3D Multimodal Large Language Models for Chemistry
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.10696v1)
- **Authors**: Lei Jiang, Shuzhou Sun, Biqing Qi, Yuchen Fu, Xiaohua Xu, Yuqiang Li, Dongzhan Zhou, Tianfan Fu
- **Abstract**: In the real world, a molecule is a 3D geometric structure. Compared to 1D SMILES sequences and 2D molecular graphs, 3D molecules represent the most informative molecular modality. Despite the rapid progress of autoregressive-based language models, they cannot handle the generation of 3D molecular conformation due to several challenges: 1) 3D molecular structures are incompatible with LLMs' discrete token space, 2) integrating heterogeneous inputs like proteins, ligands, and text remains difficult within a unified model, and 3) LLMs lack essential scientific priors, hindering the enforcement of physical and chemical constraints during generation. To tackle these issues, we present Chem3DLLM, a unified protein-conditioned multimodal large language model. Our approach designs a novel reversible text encoding for 3D molecular structures using run-length compression, achieving 3x size reduction while preserving complete structural information. This enables seamless integration of molecular geometry with protein pocket features in a single LLM architecture. We employ reinforcement learning with stability-based rewards to optimize chemical validity and incorporate a lightweight protein embedding projector for end-to-end training. Experimental results on structure-based drug design demonstrate state-of-the-art performance with a Vina score of -7.21, validating our unified multimodal approach for practical drug discovery applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Chem3DLLM: 3D Multimodal Large Language Models for Chemistry" addresses the challenges of generating 3D molecular structures using large language models (LLMs).  It introduces Chem3DLLM, a unified protein-conditioned multimodal LLM that overcomes the incompatibilities between LLMs and 3D molecular data, the difficulty of aligning multiple modalities (text, protein, ligand), and the lack of scientific priors in standard LLMs.  Chem3DLLM employs a novel reversible text encoding for 3D structures (RCMT), a protein structure projection module, and reinforcement learning with stability-based rewards (RLSF). The experimental results on structure-based drug design demonstrate state-of-the-art performance, indicating the effectiveness of the unified multimodal approach.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**

    *   The paper introduces several novel components, including the RCMT for compressing 3D molecular data, the protein projection module for multimodal alignment, and RLSF for incorporating scientific priors. The integration of these components into a single LLM architecture for 3D molecular generation represents a significant advancement.
    *   Existing approaches often treat molecular generation and protein-ligand interactions as separate tasks. Chem3DLLM's joint learning approach is more realistic and potentially more effective.
*   **Significance:**

    *   Generating 3D molecular structures is a fundamental challenge in chemistry, drug discovery, and materials science. Chem3DLLM's success in this area has important practical implications.
    *   The approach could accelerate drug discovery by enabling the design of ligands with improved binding affinity and structural validity.
    *   The methods developed in the paper could be applied to other scientific domains where 3D data is important.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the technical details of the proposed approach.
    *   The experimental results are strong, demonstrating state-of-the-art performance on the SBDD task.
    *   The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the importance of the different components of Chem3DLLM.
    *   The qualitative results illustrate the ability of Chem3DLLM to generate chemically valid and structurally plausible molecules.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The paper could benefit from a more detailed analysis of the limitations of the proposed approach.  For example, are there certain types of molecules or proteins for which Chem3DLLM performs poorly? Are there specific edge cases where RCMT or RLSF fail?
    *   While the paper mentions potential applications in other domains, it does not provide any concrete examples.
    *   The computational cost of Chem3DLLM is not discussed in detail.  This is an important consideration for practical applications.
    *   The reward function in RLSF could be improved by incorporating other factors, such as drug-likeness and synthetic accessibility. While addressed in the method, further examination is warranted.

*   **Overall:** The paper makes a significant contribution to the field of computational chemistry by introducing a novel and effective approach for generating 3D molecular structures using LLMs. The technical details are clearly explained, and the experimental results are strong. While there are some limitations, the potential impact of Chem3DLLM on drug discovery and other scientific domains is considerable.

**Score: 8.5**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a significant and innovative approach to a challenging problem in computational chemistry. It convincingly demonstrates state-of-the-art results on a key task (structure-based drug design) and introduces compelling techniques, including RCMT and RLSF, contributing to a clear advancement in the field. However, a higher score is withheld due to limitations in discussing computational costs, a more thorough exploration of limitations, and potential improvements to the reward function.

- **Classification**: cs.CE
- **Score**: 8/10

### REFN: A Reinforcement-Learning-From-Network Framework against 1-day/n-day Exploitations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.10701v1)
- **Authors**: Tianlong Yu, Lihong Liu, Ziyi Zhou, Fudu Xing, Kailong Wang, Yang Yang
- **Abstract**: The exploitation of 1 day or n day vulnerabilities poses severe threats to networked devices due to massive deployment scales and delayed patching (average Mean Time To Patch exceeds 60 days). Existing defenses, including host based patching and network based filtering, are inadequate due to limited scalability across diverse devices, compatibility issues especially with embedded or legacy systems, and error prone deployment process (manual patch validation). To address these issues, we introduce REFN (Reinforcement Learning From Network), a novel framework that trains Large Language Models (LLMs) to autonomously generate network filters to prevent 1 day or n day exploitations. REFN ensures scalability by uniquely employs Reinforcement Learning (RL) driven by online network rewards instead of traditional Human Feedback (RLHF). REFN guarantees compatibility via unified deployment on edge security gateways (Amazon Eero). REFN provides robustness via online validation using real network traffic. Crucially, REFN addresses three core challenges in training LLMs for exploit prevention: 1) expanding current LLMs limited vulnerability fixing expertise via Agentic RAG based Knowledge Distillation, 2) bridging current LLMs language to network gaps through an RL From VNF Pipeline that translates language context (vulnerability description) into network enforcement, 3) addressing the LLM hallucination and non determinism via the Online Agentic Validation that penalizes erroneous outputs. Evaluated across 22 families of 1 day or n day exploits, REFN demonstrates effectiveness (21.1 percent higher accuracy than alternatives), efficiency (Mean Time To Patch of 3.65 hours) and scalability (easily scale to 10K devices). REFN serves as an initial step toward training LLMs to rapidly prevent massive scale 1 day or n day exploitations.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces REFN, a novel reinforcement learning framework designed to train Large Language Models (LLMs) for generating network filters that mitigate 1-day/n-day vulnerabilities. REFN addresses the limitations of existing defenses (host-based patching and network-based filtering) in terms of scalability, compatibility, and error susceptibility. The core innovation lies in using Reinforcement Learning (RL) driven by real-time network rewards (from Virtualized Network Functions - VNFs), replacing traditional Human Feedback (RLHF). This allows the system to automatically adapt to evolving threats, ensures compatibility through unified deployment on edge security gateways, and provides robustness via online validation using real network traffic. REFN employs Agentic-RAG for knowledge distillation, an RL-from-VNF pipeline to bridge the language-to-network gap, and online agentic validation to penalize erroneous outputs.  Evaluations across 22 exploit families demonstrate improved accuracy, efficiency (Mean-Time-To-Patch of 3.65 hours), and scalability (easily scales to 10K devices).

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper claims significant novelty in several aspects:

*   **RL-from-Network for LLMs:**  This is a core innovation.  Leveraging real-time network rewards instead of human feedback for training LLMs in a security context is a significant departure from traditional RLHF approaches.
*   **Agentic-RAG-based Knowledge Distillation:**  While RAG and knowledge distillation are individually known, their combination with autonomous agents specifically to transfer vulnerability-fixing expertise from SOTA LLMs to more specialized models is novel.
*   **RL Dataset for Exploit Prevention:** Providing a curated dataset specifically tailored for training LLMs in this domain is a valuable contribution to the community.
*    **Framework integration:** The integration of edge gateway defense, LLMs, and VNF validation within a single framework (REFN) could be useful for practitioners

**Significance:**  The paper addresses a critical and increasingly important problem: rapid mitigation of 1-day/n-day exploits.  Existing methods are demonstrably inadequate, and the proposed framework offers a promising alternative with significant potential to impact real-world security practices.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Problem Definition:**  The paper clearly articulates the challenges associated with current vulnerability fixing strategies, highlighting their limitations in scalability, compatibility, and error-susceptibility.
*   **Well-Defined Framework:** REFN is described in detail with clear explanations of its key components (Agentic-RAG, RL-from-VNF pipeline, Online Agentic Validator) and their roles.
*   **Thorough Evaluation:**  The experimental setup is well-defined and comprehensive, comparing REFN against a range of alternative approaches across multiple metrics (FPR, FNR, Accuracy, F1-Score, Time-To-Patch, Installation Delay, Accumulative Downtime).
*   **Ablation Study:** The ablation study provides valuable insights into the contribution of each component of REFN to the overall performance, strengthening the argument for the proposed architecture.
*   **Dataset Contribution:** The release of the RL dataset is valuable and can serve as a benchmark for future research in this area.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Trust Assumptions:** The reliance on trusted edge security gateways and cloud servers represents a potential vulnerability. Compromising these components could undermine the entire defense mechanism.  This needs more discussion.
*   **Handling Encrypted Traffic:**  The paper acknowledges the limitations of the current approach in handling encrypted traffic. While the explanation of utilizing network context and potentially enterprise proxy integration is good, a more thorough exploration of alternative methods (e.g., homomorphic encryption or differential privacy) may be needed.
*   **Scalability Beyond 10k Devices:** While the paper demonstrated it scales well up to 10,000 devices, it should clarify the limitations with high device numbers. The paper also claims "easily scales to 10K devices", which seems too aggressive, as it depends on resource availability and network topology.
*   **VNF Complexity and Cost:** The paper assumes the ready availability and effective implementation of VNFs. However, developing and maintaining robust VNFs could introduce significant complexity and cost. A more detailed discussion of the practical challenges in VNF deployment and management is needed.

**Justification of Score:**

REFN presents a significant contribution to the field of network security, offering a promising approach for rapid mitigation of 1-day/n-day vulnerabilities. The use of RL with real-time network rewards, the Agentic-RAG knowledge distillation, and the online validation mechanism are novel and offer substantial improvements over existing techniques. The release of the RL dataset further strengthens the paper's impact.

However, the paper's trust assumptions and the limitations in handling encrypted traffic, potential for high VNF complexity and cost represent non-trivial drawbacks that must be acknowledged. Addressing these weaknesses is critical for widespread adoption of the REFN framework.

### Probabilistic Forecasting Method for Offshore Wind Farm Cluster under Typhoon Conditions: a Score-Based Conditional Diffusion Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.10705v1)
- **Authors**: Jinhua He, Zechun Hu
- **Abstract**: Offshore wind power (OWP) exhibits significant fluctuations under typhoon conditions, posing substantial challenges to the secure operation of power systems. Accurate forecasting of OWP is therefore essential. However, the inherent scarcity of historical typhoon data and stochasticity of OWP render traditional point forecasting methods particularly difficult and inadequate. To address this challenge and provide grid operators with the comprehensive information necessary for decision-making, this study proposes a score-based conditional diffusion model (SCDM) for probabilistic forecasting of OWP during typhoon events. First, a knowledge graph algorithm is employed to embed historical typhoon paths as vectors. Then, a deterministic network is constructed to predict the wind power under typhoon conditions based on these vector embeddings. Finally, to better characterize prediction errors, a denoising network is developed. At the core of this approach is a mean-reverting stochastic differential equation (SDE), which transforms complex error distributions into a standard Gaussian, enabling the sampling of forecasting errors using a reverse-time SDE. The probabilistic forecasting results are reconstructed by combining deterministic forecasts with sampled errors. The proposed method is evaluated using real-world data from a cluster of 9 offshore wind farms. Results demonstrate that under typhoon conditions, our approach outperforms baseline models for both deterministic and probabilistic metrics, verifying the effectiveness of the approach.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a score-based conditional diffusion model (SCDM) for probabilistic forecasting of offshore wind power (OWP) under typhoon conditions. It addresses the challenges posed by limited typhoon data and the stochastic nature of OWP during these events. The SCDM combines a knowledge graph embedding of historical typhoon paths with a deterministic network for initial wind power prediction. A denoising network is then used to model prediction errors, leveraging a mean-reverting stochastic differential equation (SDE) to transform complex error distributions into a standard Gaussian for sampling. The final probabilistic forecast is constructed by combining the deterministic forecast with sampled errors.  The method is validated on real-world data from a cluster of offshore wind farms, demonstrating improved performance compared to baseline models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the application of score-based diffusion models to the specific problem of probabilistic OWP forecasting *under typhoon conditions*.  While diffusion models are increasingly common in other generative tasks, their application to this particular problem, with the integration of knowledge graph embeddings of typhoon paths, represents a unique contribution. The use of a mean-reverting SDE tailored to the characteristics of OWP prediction errors also adds to the technical novelty. Existing studies often overlook the temporal evolution of typhoon paths, leaving a critical research gap in probabilistic WPF, this paper addresses that problem by encoding historical typhoon trajectories into feature vectors using a knowledge graph algorithm.

*   **Significance:** Accurate OWP forecasting is crucial for the reliable and secure operation of power systems, particularly in regions prone to typhoons.  By providing probabilistic forecasts, the proposed method offers grid operators a more comprehensive understanding of the potential variability in OWP generation, enabling better decision-making. The improved accuracy demonstrated in the case studies could lead to more efficient grid management and reduced reliance on backup power sources. The approach also addresses the important issue of data scarcity by leveraging historical typhoon path data via knowledge graph embeddings.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Problem Formulation:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges of OWP forecasting under typhoon conditions and motivates the need for a probabilistic approach.
    *   **Methodological Soundness:** The SCDM is well-designed and integrates several advanced techniques (knowledge graph embeddings, deterministic-probabilistic hybrid, mean-reverting SDEs) in a coherent manner.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The use of real-world data from a cluster of offshore wind farms provides strong evidence of the method's effectiveness. The comparison against multiple baseline models further strengthens the validation.
    *   **Thorough Analysis:** The paper provides a detailed analysis of the results, including a comparison of deterministic and probabilistic metrics and an assessment of the impact of typhoon path embedding.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Computational Complexity:** The paper does not explicitly address the computational cost of training and deploying the SCDM, which could be a concern for real-time applications. Details about training time, inference time, and resource requirements are missing.
    *   **Generalizability:** While the method is validated on a specific region, the generalizability to other regions with different typhoon characteristics and OWP configurations may require further investigation.
    *   **Explainability:** Diffusion models, while powerful, can be relatively opaque.  Further work could explore methods for improving the explainability of the model's predictions. The parameter tuning is heuristic, more automated parameter-tuning strategy can be explored.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field of OWP forecasting by demonstrating the effectiveness of score-based diffusion models for capturing uncertainty under extreme weather conditions. The integration of knowledge graph embeddings offers a promising approach for addressing data scarcity.  Future research could build upon this work by exploring different SDE formulations, investigating methods for improving explainability, and extending the approach to other renewable energy sources and extreme weather events.

*   **Justification of Score:**

Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, the paper warrants a score of 7. The integration of a knowledge graph for typhoon path embedding into a diffusion model tailored for offshore wind power forecasting under typhoon conditions is a genuinely novel application. It addresses a real-world problem of significant importance. The experimental results are strong, demonstrating clear improvements over baseline methods. However, the computational complexity is not addressed, and the generalizability and explainability could be further explored. The work represents a solid contribution to the field, even if further work is needed to address the points raised above.

### CountCluster: Training-Free Object Quantity Guidance with Cross-Attention Map Clustering for Text-to-Image Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.10710v1)
- **Authors**: Joohyeon Lee, Jin-Seop Lee, Jee-Hyong Lee
- **Abstract**: Diffusion-based text-to-image generation models have demonstrated strong performance in terms of image quality and diversity. However, they still struggle to generate images that accurately reflect the number of objects specified in the input prompt. Several approaches have been proposed that rely on either external counting modules for iterative refinement or quantity representations derived from learned tokens or latent features. However, they still have limitations in accurately reflecting the specified number of objects and overlook an important structural characteristic--The number of object instances in the generated image is largely determined in the early timesteps of the denoising process. To correctly reflect the object quantity for image generation, the highly activated regions in the object cross-attention map at the early timesteps should match the input object quantity, while each region should be clearly separated. To address this issue, we propose \textit{CountCluster}, a method that guides the object cross-attention map to be clustered according to the specified object count in the input, without relying on any external tools or additional training. The proposed method partitions the object cross-attention map into $k$ clusters at inference time based on attention scores, defines an ideal distribution in which each cluster is spatially well-separated, and optimizes the latent to align with this target distribution. Our method achieves an average improvement of 18.5\%p in object count accuracy compared to existing methods, and demonstrates superior quantity control performance across a variety of prompts. Code will be released at: https://github.com/JoohyeonL22/CountCluster .
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CountCluster, a novel, training-free approach to improve object quantity control in text-to-image diffusion models.  The method focuses on manipulating the cross-attention maps associated with object tokens early in the denoising process.  It works by clustering regions within the cross-attention map based on attention scores, defining a target spatial distribution that encourages well-separated clusters, and then optimizing the latent representation to align with this target distribution. By ensuring the number of attention clusters matches the specified object count and that these clusters are spatially distinct, the method aims to generate images that accurately reflect the desired quantity of objects. The approach avoids reliance on external tools or additional training, offering efficiency and ease of applicability. The authors demonstrate improved object count accuracy compared to existing methods on a variety of prompts.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a reasonably novel approach.  While latent optimization based on attention maps is not entirely new, the specific focus on early-stage cross-attention map clustering and the design of a target spatial distribution to control object quantity is a distinctive contribution. The training-free nature of the method is also noteworthy, offering advantages over techniques that require additional training or external modules.

*   **Significance:** The problem of accurate object quantity control in text-to-image generation is a recognized limitation of current diffusion models. The paper addresses this limitation with a relatively efficient and accessible method. The improvements in object count accuracy demonstrated in the experiments are significant and suggest the potential for the proposed technique to be adopted and integrated into existing diffusion pipelines. However, the improvement isn't overwhelmingly large to the point where every researcher in the field will have to immediately adopt this technique.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Training-free and efficient, making it easily adaptable to existing models.
    *   Focus on early-stage attention map manipulation is well-reasoned and effective.
    *   Clear and well-motivated method with a practical objective.
    *   Demonstrated improvement in quantitative and qualitative results compared to baselines.
    *   Provides code for reproducibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The ablation studies, while present, could be more extensive, exploring the sensitivity to various parameters and design choices more thoroughly.
    *   The reliance on Gaussian smoothing and min-max normalization adds hyperparameter complexity that needs further investigation to assure generalisability.
    *   The evaluation focuses primarily on relatively simple prompts.  While complex prompts are included in the qualitative results, a more robust quantitative analysis of the method's performance on diverse and complex scene descriptions would strengthen the paper.
    *   The reliance on counting models like CountGD for quantitative evaluation introduces potential biases or inaccuracies related to the limitations of the counting models themselves. The authors do acknowledge the limitations of VQA-guided evaluation, but a deeper discussion of potential biases in automated count estimation would be valuable. The TIFA study shows lower "appearing" scores which point to the need for improved details.
    *   Inference time is increased, a trade-off for more accuracy, but could present challenges for real-time applications.
    * The implementation details section, whilst complete, does not feature an error and analysis and what were the impacts. This can be a good way to improve the manuscript and add in extra depth.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper has a solid chance of influencing future research in text-to-image generation.  Its simplicity, effectiveness, and training-free nature could lead to its adoption as a baseline or component in other methods aimed at improving object control. It may also inspire new approaches to latent optimization and attention map manipulation.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper provides a novel and effective approach to a real-world problem in a timely manner. There are some areas of improvement that are necessary, as mentioned above. The improvements in accuracy are significant, the proposed framework is solid, and the ablation study and qualitative assessment enhance the rigour.

### NextStep-1: Toward Autoregressive Image Generation with Continuous Tokens at Scale
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.10711v1)
- **Authors**: NextStep Team, Chunrui Han, Guopeng Li, Jingwei Wu, Quan Sun, Yan Cai, Yuang Peng, Zheng Ge, Deyu Zhou, Haomiao Tang, Hongyu Zhou, Kenkun Liu, Ailin Huang, Bin Wang, Changxin Miao, Deshan Sun, En Yu, Fukun Yin, Gang Yu, Hao Nie, Haoran Lv, Hanpeng Hu, Jia Wang, Jian Zhou, Jianjian Sun, Kaijun Tan, Kang An, Kangheng Lin, Liang Zhao, Mei Chen, Peng Xing, Rui Wang, Shiyu Liu, Shutao Xia, Tianhao You, Wei Ji, Xianfang Zeng, Xin Han, Xuelin Zhang, Yana Wei, Yanming Xu, Yimin Jiang, Yingming Wang, Yu Zhou, Yucheng Han, Ziyang Meng, Binxing Jiao, Daxin Jiang, Xiangyu Zhang, Yibo Zhu
- **Abstract**: Prevailing autoregressive (AR) models for text-to-image generation either rely on heavy, computationally-intensive diffusion models to process continuous image tokens, or employ vector quantization (VQ) to obtain discrete tokens with quantization loss. In this paper, we push the autoregressive paradigm forward with NextStep-1, a 14B autoregressive model paired with a 157M flow matching head, training on discrete text tokens and continuous image tokens with next-token prediction objectives. NextStep-1 achieves state-of-the-art performance for autoregressive models in text-to-image generation tasks, exhibiting strong capabilities in high-fidelity image synthesis. Furthermore, our method shows strong performance in image editing, highlighting the power and versatility of our unified approach. To facilitate open research, we will release our code and models to the community.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces NextStep-1, a 14B parameter autoregressive model for text-to-image generation.  It leverages a flow matching head (157M parameters) to predict continuous image tokens conditioned on discrete text tokens. This approach aims to bridge the gap between autoregressive models and computationally intensive diffusion models, as well as overcoming the limitations of VQ-based discrete tokenization.  The model achieves state-of-the-art results for autoregressive models on various benchmarks, demonstrating strong capabilities in image synthesis, editing, and compositional understanding. The authors emphasize the simplicity and effectiveness of their next-token prediction paradigm and promise to release code and models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents an incremental, rather than revolutionary, advancement. While autoregressive models for image generation exist, the combination of a large transformer backbone, a flow matching head for continuous tokens, and careful engineering of the image tokenizer appears novel. However, the core building blocks (autoregressive modeling, flow matching, VAE tokenizers) are well-established. The innovation lies in the specific architecture and training regime.

*   **Significance:** The paper’s significance hinges on several factors:

    *   **Performance Improvement:** The claim of achieving state-of-the-art *autoregressive* performance in text-to-image generation is significant *if* validated by independent replication. Surpassing previous autoregressive approaches demonstrates a viable alternative to diffusion models which is important for efficiency. However, claiming *overall* state-of-the-art (without strongly surpassing diffusion models) is questionable.

    *   **Simplicity and Scalability:** The authors emphasize the simplicity of their approach. If the method is indeed easier to implement and scale compared to diffusion models, it could have a tangible impact on the accessibility of high-quality image generation.

    *   **Technical Contributions:** The paper's analysis of the relationship between the AR Transformer and the Flow Matching Head, and insights into training VAE-based autoregressive models with high dimensional latent spaces are valuable. The demonstration of channel-wise normalization for handling issues arising with CFG are important findings.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear and well-structured presentation.
    *   Comprehensive empirical evaluation with several standard benchmarks.
    *   Discussion of limitations and challenges offers transparency.
    *   The architectural choices are well-justified.
    *   Offers practical insights into training such a large model.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Incremental novelty compared to existing techniques.
    *   Relies on pre-trained components (Qwen2.5, Flux VAE), reducing the paper's self-containedness.
    *   It's unclear whether the "state-of-the-art" performance claimed is maintained with independent validation or holds against the latest diffusion models released since the submission of the paper.
    *   The claim that it outperforms diffusion models is too strong; the performance is comparable.

*   **Potential Influence:** If the promised code and model release materialize, this work could influence future research in autoregressive image generation by providing a strong baseline. The insights into tokenizer design and handling instability under strong CFG could be valuable to the community. It could encourage a re-evaluation of the autoregressive approach, potentially leading to new architectures and training techniques. However, its impact will be limited if it doesn't significantly outperform diffusion methods in terms of either quality or efficiency.

**Rigorous Rationale:**
The paper presents a valuable contribution by effectively combining existing components (Transformers, flow matching, VAE) into a high-performing autoregressive image generation system. The empirical results show its strong performance, while the analysis and insights gained during development give important context for future work. However, given the somewhat incremental nature of the method relative to the existing literature, and the fact that the model’s advantages over diffusion models (if any) are only marginal, I am assigning a score of 7. It contributes to the field but stops short of being a groundbreaking or paradigm-shifting paper.

### Exploiting Discriminative Codebook Prior for Autoregressive Image Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.10719v1)
- **Authors**: Longxiang Tang, Ruihang Chu, Xiang Wang, Yujin Han, Pingyu Wu, Chunming He, Yingya Zhang, Shiwei Zhang, Jiaya Jia
- **Abstract**: Advanced discrete token-based autoregressive image generation systems first tokenize images into sequences of token indices with a codebook, and then model these sequences in an autoregressive paradigm. While autoregressive generative models are trained only on index values, the prior encoded in the codebook, which contains rich token similarity information, is not exploited. Recent studies have attempted to incorporate this prior by performing naive k-means clustering on the tokens, helping to facilitate the training of generative models with a reduced codebook. However, we reveal that k-means clustering performs poorly in the codebook feature space due to inherent issues, including token space disparity and centroid distance inaccuracy. In this work, we propose the Discriminative Codebook Prior Extractor (DCPE) as an alternative to k-means clustering for more effectively mining and utilizing the token similarity information embedded in the codebook. DCPE replaces the commonly used centroid-based distance, which is found to be unsuitable and inaccurate for the token feature space, with a more reasonable instance-based distance. Using an agglomerative merging technique, it further addresses the token space disparity issue by avoiding splitting high-density regions and aggregating low-density ones. Extensive experiments demonstrate that DCPE is plug-and-play and integrates seamlessly with existing codebook prior-based paradigms. With the discriminative prior extracted, DCPE accelerates the training of autoregressive models by 42% on LlamaGen-B and improves final FID and IS performance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses a weakness in discrete token-based autoregressive image generation:  the underutilization of similarity information encoded within the codebook.  Current methods often rely on naive k-means clustering to group tokens, which the authors argue is suboptimal due to "token space disparity" (non-uniform token density) and "centroid distance inaccuracy" in the token feature space.  They propose the "Discriminative Codebook Prior Extractor" (DCPE) as an alternative.  DCPE uses agglomerative clustering with instance-based distance measures to better capture token relationships.  Experiments show DCPE accelerates training and improves the quality of generated images compared to k-means based approaches and can be integrated as a plug-and-play component into existing frameworks like LlamaGen, CTF, and IAR.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of exploiting token similarity information isn't entirely novel; previous works like IAR and CTF have explored this. However, the specific criticism of k-means' suitability in the token feature space and the proposed DCPE as a more appropriate alternative have significant novelty. The identification and characterization of token space disparity and centroid distance inaccuracy is a valuable contribution. The core idea of DCPE using agglomerative clustering with instance based distance is moderately novel, although agglomerative clustering is a well-established method. The design choices for DCPE seem well reasoned for the token feature space.
*   **Significance:** The significance lies in addressing a practical limitation in autoregressive image generation. By improving the extraction of codebook priors, the authors enable faster training and potentially better-quality image generation. The plug-and-play nature of DCPE allows for easy adoption by existing methods. It reduces the parameter count needed for generating images.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and well-motivated arguments.
    *   Ablation studies to justify design choices (agglomerative clustering, instance-based distance).
    *   Comprehensive experiments showing improvements on multiple benchmarks and with different architectures (LlamaGen, CTF, IAR, GigaTok).
    *   The method is demonstrated to be plug-and-play and can be integrated with existing frameworks.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the performance is improved with respect to FID and IS, the other metrics are not always consistently better, and a proper analysis that addresses such variations is missing.
    *   The approach relies on average linkage, which could still face issues with chaining and sensitivity to noise. The claim of "preserving high-density regions" might not always hold if two relatively dense regions are further apart than a very sparse region to a single dense region. This aspect could benefit from more analysis.
    *   The claim that DCPE does not guarantee a globally optimal solution is a major problem with all agglomerative clustering, so it is not a weakness of the specific method.
    *   The gains are incremental. The improvement in FID/IS compared to the baseline is noticeable but not earth-shattering. It's a significant and useful step, but not a fundamental breakthrough.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the design of future autoregressive image generation methods. By advocating for more careful consideration of the codebook's feature space and offering a practical alternative to k-means, it can guide researchers toward more effective ways to leverage codebook priors. The ease of integration with other methods amplifies its potential impact.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of autoregressive image generation by identifying and addressing a weakness in existing methods. The proposal of DCPE, along with thorough experiments and ablation studies, showcases its effectiveness. However, the gains are somewhat incremental, and there are some limitations in the methodology. The approach is definitely innovative and solves a crucial problem. It is plug-and-play and can improve future generation methods.

### EgoCross: Benchmarking Multimodal Large Language Models for Cross-Domain Egocentric Video Question Answering
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.10729v1)
- **Authors**: Yanjun Li, Yuqian Fu, Tianwen Qian, Qi'ao Xu, Silong Dai, Danda Pani Paudel, Luc Van Gool, Xiaoling Wang
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have significantly pushed the frontier of egocentric video question answering (EgocentricQA). However, existing benchmarks and studies are mainly limited to common daily activities such as cooking and cleaning. In contrast, real-world deployment inevitably encounters domain shifts, where target domains differ substantially in both visual style and semantic content. To bridge this gap, we introduce \textbf{EgoCross}, a comprehensive benchmark designed to evaluate the cross-domain generalization of MLLMs in EgocentricQA. EgoCross covers four diverse and challenging domains, including surgery, industry, extreme sports, and animal perspective, representing realistic and high-impact application scenarios. It comprises approximately 1,000 QA pairs across 798 video clips, spanning four key QA tasks: prediction, recognition, localization, and counting. Each QA pair provides both OpenQA and CloseQA formats to support fine-grained evaluation. Extensive experiments show that most existing MLLMs, whether general-purpose or egocentric-specialized, struggle to generalize to domains beyond daily life, highlighting the limitations of current models. Furthermore, we conduct several pilot studies, \eg, fine-tuning and reinforcement learning, to explore potential improvements. We hope EgoCross and our accompanying analysis will serve as a foundation for advancing domain-adaptive, robust egocentric video understanding. Data and codes will be released at: \href{https://github.com/MyUniverse0726/EgoCross}{https://github.com/MyUniverse0726/EgoCross.}
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "EgoCross: Benchmarking Multimodal Large Language Models for Cross-Domain Egocentric Video Question Answering":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EgoCross, a new benchmark dataset designed to evaluate the cross-domain generalization capabilities of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) in the context of egocentric video question answering (EgocentricQA).  The dataset comprises approximately 1,000 question-answer pairs across 798 video clips spanning four diverse domains: surgery, industry, extreme sports, and animal perspectives.  These domains are chosen to represent realistic and high-impact application scenarios that go beyond the typical daily-life activities commonly addressed in existing EgocentricQA benchmarks. The benchmark includes four key QA tasks: prediction, recognition, localization, and counting, with both OpenQA and CloseQA formats provided for fine-grained evaluation. Experimental results show that existing MLLMs struggle to generalize to these domains, highlighting limitations of current models. The paper also includes pilot studies exploring potential improvements through fine-tuning and reinforcement learning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the **introduction of a cross-domain EgocentricQA benchmark**. Existing benchmarks are heavily focused on common daily activities, leaving a significant gap in evaluating the robustness and generalization ability of MLLMs to more specialized and real-world application domains. The inclusion of domains like surgery and industry is a definite step up in complexity and practical relevance. The dual OpenQA/CloseQA format is also a useful feature for comprehensive evaluation. However, the tasks themselves (prediction, recognition, localization, counting) are not novel in isolation; their novelty stems from their application in the chosen diverse domains.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a **crucial limitation** of current MLLMs for egocentric vision: their poor generalization performance across different domains. The EgoCross benchmark provides a valuable tool for researchers to develop and evaluate models that are more robust and adaptable to real-world deployment scenarios. The pilot studies, while preliminary, offer potential directions for future research. The significance is amplified by the practical nature of the chosen domains, indicating the potential for impact in areas like healthcare, industrial automation, and assistive technologies.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-Motivated:** The paper clearly articulates the need for a cross-domain benchmark and provides compelling justifications for the choice of domains.
    *   **Comprehensive Benchmark Design:** The benchmark incorporates a variety of QA tasks and provides both OpenQA and CloseQA formats, enabling a thorough evaluation of model capabilities.
    *   **Systematic Evaluation:** The paper presents a comprehensive evaluation of several state-of-the-art MLLMs, revealing their limitations in cross-domain generalization.
    *   **Pilot Studies:** The inclusion of pilot studies exploring potential improvement strategies adds value to the paper.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Pilot Studies:** The pilot studies are preliminary and could benefit from more in-depth analysis and exploration.
    *   **Dataset Size:** While the dataset is a valuable contribution, a larger dataset with more diverse video content could further enhance the benchmark's effectiveness. ~1k QA pairs is a decent starting point, but expanding this would be impactful.
    *   **Lack of Novel Models:** The paper primarily focuses on benchmarking existing models rather than proposing a novel model specifically designed for cross-domain EgocentricQA. This is understandable, as the primary goal is to establish the benchmark, but it slightly reduces the overall impact.
    *   **LLM-as-judge concerns:** relying heavily on the LLM-as-judge paradigm can introduce bias to the evaluation since these models can be prone to hallucination or just plain error.

*   **Potential Influence:** EgoCross has the potential to become a standard benchmark for evaluating the cross-domain generalization capabilities of MLLMs in EgocentricQA. It can drive research towards developing more robust and adaptable models, ultimately leading to more practical applications of egocentric vision technology.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of egocentric video understanding by addressing a significant gap in current research: the lack of benchmarks for cross-domain generalization. The EgoCross dataset is well-designed and motivated, and the comprehensive evaluation of existing MLLMs provides valuable insights into their limitations. While the pilot studies are preliminary and the dataset size could be larger, the paper's strengths outweigh its weaknesses. The EgoCross benchmark has the potential to significantly influence future research in this area. However, the reliance on LLM-as-judge for open-ended QA, coupled with the benchmark dataset's somewhat limited size in comparison to benchmarks in other domains, prevents it from earning a very high score.

### Thinking Inside the Mask: In-Place Prompting in Diffusion LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.10736v1)
- **Authors**: Xiangqi Jin, Yuxuan Wang, Yifeng Gao, Zichen Wen, Biqing Qi, Dongrui Liu, Linfeng Zhang
- **Abstract**: Despite large language models (LLMs) have achieved remarkable success, their prefix-only prompting paradigm and sequential generation process offer limited flexibility for bidirectional information. Diffusion large language models (dLLMs) present new opportunities through their bidirectional attention mechanisms and iterative refinement processes, enabling more flexible in-place prompting strategies. We introduce ICE (In-Place Chain-of-Thought Prompting with Early Exit), a novel framework that transforms prefix-only prompting into in-place prompting specifically designed for dLLMs. ICE integrates in-place prompts directly within masked token positions during iterative refinement and employs a confidence-aware early exit mechanism to significantly reduce computational overhead. Extensive experiments demonstrate ICE's effectiveness, achieving up to 17.29% accuracy improvement with 4.12$\times$ speedup on GSM8K, and up to 276.67$\times$ acceleration on MMLU while maintaining competitive performance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "Thinking Inside the Mask: In-Place Prompting in Diffusion LLMs" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ICE (In-Place Chain-of-Thought Prompting with Early Exit), a novel framework designed to enhance reasoning and inference efficiency in diffusion large language models (dLLMs).  ICE leverages the bidirectional attention mechanisms and iterative refinement processes inherent in dLLMs to implement in-place prompting, where reasoning steps are directly embedded within masked token positions during generation.  It also uses a confidence-aware early exit mechanism, significantly reducing computational overhead by stopping the iterative refinement process early when the model demonstrates high confidence in its answer.  Experimental results on GSM8K, MATH, MMLU, and GPQA demonstrate ICE's effectiveness in improving both accuracy and speed.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of in-place prompting is novel, and the application to diffusion LMs is significant. It is a departure from traditional prefix-based prompting that has been highly successful in autoregressive models. Embedding the reasoning steps within the generation itself, leveraging the bidirectional nature of dLLMs, is a genuinely interesting architectural innovation. The confidence-aware early exit mechanism is also a well-motivated contribution. The idea is grounded in the observation that dLLMs often converge on the answer before completely refining the reasoning trace, offering significant performance efficiency benefits. However, the approach is highly related to a line of work called "inner monologue", and the use of intermediate steps for reasoning has been heavily explored. Therefore, the overall novelty has some limits.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical problem in dLLMs: their high computational cost due to iterative generation.  Improving reasoning capabilities *and* inference efficiency is a valuable contribution, potentially making dLLMs more practical for real-world applications.  The experimental results strongly support the claim that ICE achieves both goals.  The significant speedups on tasks like MMLU and GPQA are particularly impressive. However, there is no explanation on the selection of the length of the blocks of tokens to mask.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-Motivated:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of prefix-only prompting in autoregressive models and how dLLMs offer an opportunity to overcome these limitations.
    *   **Technically Sound:** The ICE framework is well-described, and the design choices (in-place prompting, two-phase decoding, confidence-aware early exit) are justified.
    *   **Strong Empirical Validation:** The experimental results across multiple benchmarks are convincing, demonstrating both accuracy improvements and significant speedups. The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of different components of the ICE framework.
    *   **Practicality:** The ICE framework is shown to be compatible with existing acceleration techniques such as dLLM-Cache, further enhancing its practical value.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The paper alludes to the importance of hyperparameter tuning (confidence threshold, reasoning steps), but more details on how to effectively tune these parameters would be helpful. Further analysis of the sensitivity to block sizes could also be performed.
    *   **Limited Baseline Comparison:** Comparing against a more extensive set of recent and relevant methods that focus on efficient dLLM inference, especially in reasoning tasks, could strengthen the results. The baseline used, vanilla dLLMs, could benefit from further refinement (e.g., the specific details of prompting employed).
    *   **Lack of Theoretical Analysis:** While the empirical results are impressive, a more rigorous theoretical analysis of the ICE framework's convergence properties and computational complexity could further enhance the paper's contribution.

*   **Potential Influence:**  ICE has the potential to influence future research in dLLMs by:

    *   Inspiring new approaches to prompt engineering that leverage bidirectional attention and iterative refinement.
    *   Encouraging the development of more efficient inference techniques for dLLMs.
    *   Providing a foundation for building dLLMs with enhanced reasoning capabilities.
    *   The code release will make the approach more attractive to the community.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the dLLM research area. The in-place prompting and early exit mechanisms represent a genuinely novel approach to leveraging the unique advantages of diffusion models. The empirical results are compelling, showing significant performance gains across several benchmarks. The clear motivation and detailed explanation of the framework enhance the paper's value. However, the hyperparameter sensitivity, limited baseline comparison, and the lack of a formal theoretical analysis hold back its overall significance.
